# BLACKBOARD — w2m

*The project's working record. Seven sections. Single-writer per section. Everything written here that matters to the Architect was also said in chat in the same turn. Template: `sdd-kit-3/templates/BLACKBOARD.md`.*

---

## Surfaced for review

- **2026-10-08 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (after vocabulary bumps 0.2, 0.3, 0.4; hard rule 5, overdue since 0.2 per the project review): The vocabulary in force is `signals/0.4.json`, 60 tags in 12 categories on two planes. The harness plane is substrate events whose kind is the Struct class name; the guest plane is content-addressed JSONL traces ordered by (thread_id, thread_seq, hb_refs). Since 0.1: harness integers are bounded to ±(2^53−1) for RFC 8785 (P8); capability values name the 4 KB IPA granule, guest TSO, and three 4 KB protection paths (P9, P10); 0.4 renames `objective_ref` (no money word in an optimization target), adds `not_established` so a probe that cannot decide says so, and lets imported executions omit what was not observed, naming it in `unobserved`. What the project is for has not changed: contracts first, generated implementations verified against them, a Windows game launched as a Mac app (v0.2 §1). What has fired: 14 of 60 tags; none of generation, model checking, reduction, or discrepancy routing. That gap is the next work (Decision 2026-10-08, sequence).
- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION: This project is w2m, a system meant to take the Windows x86-64 distribution of a Steam game and produce a Mac-native application from the binaries alone, governed by a versioned, executable Windows execution contract (`windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_1.md` §2-3). The review at `docs/review-2026-10-03-research-direction-v0_1.md` narrows v0 to one DRM-free D3D11 game in a Hypervisor.framework VM backend using the macOS 26 4 KB IPA granule and hardware TSO, on this host (macOS 26.5.1, M5 Max, 16 KB host pages). SDD at root: the vocabulary is the contract, signals are designed typed events and not logs, and the lossy step being removed is a human describing why a game misbehaves — here the program's own trace (contract id, minimized program, mismatch class) replaces it, which is the research direction's §11 discrepancy report expressed as signals. The loop: write a card, self-review it, execute, run the gates (`sdd-kit-3/AGENTS.md` dual contract plus observation contract for anything that runs guest code), write the Signal Report, run the Rubber Duck Pass against `signals/0.1.json`, append to this file per single-writer discipline. Resolve-and-record: questions the project, the kit, or a named engineering source settle get decided and written to `## Decisions` with grounds; only the closed user-only list (here: which game, the Windows oracle hardware, Steam credentials, licensing intent) is asked, in chat, while independent work continues. No implementation sprint dispatches before `signals/0.1.json` and its rationale are signed (hard rule 12); `sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md` is that session.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — User-only questions asked in chat, each with a recommendation: (Q1) first target game; (Q2) Windows x86-64 oracle machine; (Q3) Steam credentials for DepotDownloader, deferred to the acquisition phase; (Q4) commercial intent, given substrate's PolyForm Noncommercial license. Sprint 0 proceeds on recommended answers marked provisional.

- **2026-10-03 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (re-articulated after the v0.2 scope change): The founding document is now `windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_2.md`. Its first-class subsystems are contract construction from three evidence categories (documented, observed on a named reference profile, hypothesis; §11.1), the specification compiler and the application compiler joined by one versioned contract (§2), code discovery with validity and invalidation (§5.1), the versioned Steam bridge (§4.2), and substrate as the orchestration harness whose append-only record does not serialize guest execution (§11.4). The plan leaves the CPU backend, first game, first graphics profile, and shader-converter adoption open, so this session's earlier VM-first and DRM-free calls no longer stand. Each becomes a recorded decision backed by capability probes and conformance results (§8.2, §15). Two repair paths stay separate: implementation repair against an unchanged contract, and contract revision with evidence and dependency invalidation (§11.3). The vocabulary has to carry both, so no candidate can make itself pass by changing its expected output. Sprint 0 (`sprints/sprint-000-vocabulary-session.md`) is retargeted to v0.2 and stays the next work.

---
- **2026-10-09 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: vocabulary 0.5 in force, the x86-64 profile changed, the architecture decided, 32-bit in scope; review 2026-10-09 progress K2): w2m runs a Windows x86-64 Steam game on this Mac inside a Hypervisor.framework VM that enforces x86 ordering and 4 KB pages in hardware. The guest's Windows-visible state (TEB, PEB, CONTEXT, a per-thread state block) lives in guest memory at Windows layouts; Windows services are freestanding C inside the guest, each a compilation unit generated against a contract and judged against Windows; x86 code is decoded by a spec-generated decoder judged by XED, lifted to explicit operations judged by a real x86 CPU, and compiled to AArch64. The x86-64 profile is Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono: its logic is code generated at run time); BioShock 2 Remastered is the 32-bit profile. Vocabulary 0.5 (61 tags, two planes) names every event; a substrate run holds the chain from cause to verdict, and the records under store/runs/ replay. The plan's current order lives in docs/plan.md.

- **2026-10-09 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: vocabulary 0.7 in force since Sprint 039; kit reread in full after compaction): w2m runs Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono, x86-64) on this M5 Max inside a Hypervisor.framework VM; x86 is decoded by a spec judged by XED, lifted to explicit operations judged instruction by instruction against Intel CPUs (39,708 cases in Sprint 039, faults included), and compiled to AArch64; Windows services are freestanding C in the guest. Vocabulary 0.7 (P17 added six FP capability values) names every event on two planes; each sprint is judged by a reference outside w2m (Windows on the runner, the CPU, XED) with planted faults that must be caught. Tonight's user decisions bind the work: blocks are removed, not routed around; a test that cannot run means the sprints are out of order, so Sprint 040 is judged on Sprint 039's Windows fault records and the scheduler moved to 047; nothing local takes seconds. `docs/plan.md` holds the order.

- **2026-10-16 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: the full review of 2026-10-16 made `docs/plan.md`'s seven-step order the roadmap; kit and project SDD reread in full): w2m runs Hail to the Rainbow (Unity 2019.4, Mono, x86-64) inside a Hypervisor.framework VM on this M5 Max, its services in the guest runtime's C, its x86 compiled to AArch64; vocabulary 0.7 (61 tags, two planes) names every event, and each sprint is judged by a reference outside w2m (the families' Windows rows, the Xeon's class cases, the game's own call stream from the laptop) with planted faults that must be caught, closing on green CI of its closing commit. Sprint 094 measured the start: 48.7 and 44.3 s for 100 billion instructions, the main thread retiring 63.8% of them with 233.6 million PAUSEs. Step 2 now: the scheduler already switches, sleeps and warps inside the VM (Sprints 069, 070; 997,529 switches in the VM, 2 hand-overs in the host), so what remains is measured, not assumed: a release that readies every waiter on its key where one can take it (`w2m_signal`, `LeaveCriticalSection`, `WakeConditionVariable`), and spinning that burns a slice on one vCPU. SDD gaps found in the reread and fixed in Sprint 095's commit: `## Built` stops at Sprint 066, and `## Sprint tail` holds about 60 entries against the kit's 10.
- **2026-10-22 agent** — COMPREHENSION_AFFIRMATION (hard rule 5: the scope change of 2026-10-21, w2m rewritten in Rust only; sdd-kit-3 reread in full, 47 files): w2m still runs Hail to the Rainbow (app 1699230, build 24247624) on this M5 Max inside a Hypervisor.framework VM, toward milestones B and C; only the language and the host's shape change. The Python host could not serve vCPUs in parallel under the GIL, and no test of its design against the game's 37,600 calls a second came before Sprints 107 to 122 (docs/postmortem-2026-10-21.md). The order is `docs/rust-port-plan.md`: freeze the oracle, a design, a Rust host on one vCPU forwarding unported exits to Python, then exit kinds, the translator, loading, the model, several vCPUs, the guest runtime, the compiler in the VM, the judges. What does not change is the contract: vocabulary 0.9 (`signals/current.json`), the judges as gates (the batch run's 168 lines, 456 of 458 classes, 68 of 68 families, stencil_judge.py's four judges), `scripts/gates.sh` and green CI on the closing commit, one concept per card, every agent decision recorded here with its grounds. Rust code speaks the vocabulary through generated types with a parity check (Decision 2026-10-22).

## Decisions

- **2026-10-03 user** — Project scope: build the system described in `windows_to_macos_research_direction_v0_1.md` (pasted in chat 2026-10-03): obtain a Windows Steam game's distribution from the Mac command line, prepare it automatically, and launch it as a native Mac application; original Windows binaries are the input; Wine, Rosetta, and D3DMetal are not architectural dependencies. Use the substrate repo (`https://github.com/laffeyp/substrate`) and the SDD kit (`sdd-kit-3/`). Instruction verbatim: "use this, read the sdd kit in full, to review this and prep to make it."
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — What role substrate plays. Decision: substrate is the generation-and-verification harness (research direction §1 "the project's own generation and automated verification system"): contract implementations are generated and checked through `best_of_n_verified` / `coding_flow`-shaped topologies, and every probe, generation, and conformance run is a substrate run record. Substrate is not the compiler or the guest runtime. Grounds: project — research direction §1, §11 and substrate `docs/applications.md`; SDD — `ADDENDUMS.md` Addendum D (discovery harness built on substrate); practice — substrate `docs/api.md` (`embedded_substrate`, `assert_event`, `first_divergence`). Revisit if: the user meant substrate as code to fork, or as the shipped runtime.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — The Windows execution contract is mined from a Windows oracle by probes; Microsoft documentation is a hint, not the source of truth. Grounds: project — review §1; SDD — `grammar/BOOTSTRAP.md` anti-pattern "fabricating to avoid a gap"; practice — Wine project history (1993 to date) of undocumented-behavior dependence. Revisit if: a contract built from documentation alone passes a real game's observation contract.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — First CPU backend is a Hypervisor.framework VM (4 KB IPA granule, TSOEN) with a compact guest runtime; native-process lowering comes later, measured against it. Grounds: project — review §2.2, host macOS 26.5.1 / M5 Max; SDD — TECHNIQUES.md #43 (refactor as behavior-preserving chain, so the second backend is checked against the first); practice — Apple Hypervisor updates (macOS 26 `hv_vm_config_set_ipa_granule`), Linux TSO patch series 2024-04 (TSOEN modifiable from macOS 14.4). Revisit if: a public native-process TSO path is found, or VM-exit cost on the graphics/audio boundary exceeds the frame budget.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — Dynamic x86-64 translation is a day-one correctness component; AOT is a verified cache over it. Grounds: project — review §2.1; SDD — none specific; practice — static code/data separation is undecidable in general (Horspool and Marovac, 1980); Mono JIT and LuaJIT in shipped games. Revisit if: never for correctness; AOT-only may be an optimization mode per game.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — v0 excludes Denuvo, kernel-mode anti-cheat, and D3D12; SteamStub is in scope only via dynamic unpacking. Grounds: project — review §2.3, §5; SDD — hard rule 6 (one concept at a time, applied at phase scale); practice — DXMT and Metal Shader Converter coverage (DXBC vs DXIL). Revisit if: the user's chosen first game needs one of them.
- **2026-10-03 agent-resolved** — Project layout: the kit lives at `sdd-kit-3/` (read-only); project artifacts at the root per `sdd-kit-3/README.md` "Project layout after adoption"; the research direction stays at the root unedited; reviews and specs under `docs/`. Grounds: project — existing files at `/Users/peterlaffey/port`; SDD — README layout, hard rule 12 (no deletions); practice — n/a. Revisit if: a git remote is created with a different layout.

Decision: (1) test profile: BioShock 2 Remastered, Steam app 409720 (2016; Steam store: 64-bit only, DirectX 11, single-player, no third-party DRM disclosed; already in this Mac's Steam library as the Mac build, so the user owns it, and its native Mac port is a §12.2 comparison subject); (2) modern profile: The Blood of Dawnwalker, Steam app 3751260 (released 2026-09-02/03; Unreal Engine 5; Steam store: DirectX 12, 64-bit, single-player, no third-party DRM disclosed; developer Rebel Wolves confirmed no Denuvo per MP1st and PurePC reports). - **2026-10-05 agent-resolved** — Windows reference machine (under the Q2 delegation). Decision: the modal configuration in the Steam Hardware & Software Survey, September 2026: Windows 11 64-bit (71.34% of systems), 8 physical CPU cores (30.06%), 32 GB RAM (42.22%), NVIDIA GeForce RTX 5070-class GPU (most common card; two reads of the survey page gave 5.86% and 6.15%), 1920x1080 display (47.91%). Native x86-64 only; Windows on Arm is excluded as a reference (v0.2 §11.1). Clean Windows 11 install with the build pinned and recorded in the reference profile. CPU vendor (Intel or AMD) is recorded as part of the profile; the survey's vendor split was not read successfully, so either vendor is acceptable for the first profile and the other becomes a second profile later. Grounds: project — v0.2 §11.1 reference-profile fields; SDD — Addendum B C1 (one dedicated device addressed by id, not a shared machine); practice — store.steampowered.com/hwsurvey read 2026-10-05. Revisit if: the machine on hand differs; the profile records what it is, and the choice of first vendor moves to evidence.

- **2026-10-05 agent-resolved** — Vocabulary Session (Sprint 000) decisions, ten entries, each argued in `signals/0.1-rationale.md` "Agent-resolved decisions": (1) tag name == substrate Struct class name == event kind, no mapping table; (2) two transports: substrate bus for harness tags, content-addressed JSONL files for `guest` tags; (3) guest order is (thread_id, thread_seq, hb_refs), never substrate seq; (4) strict validator extras on both planes; (5) `windows_arm_emulated` is a recordable profile_kind that constraint E4 bars from native x86 evidence; (6) graphics-specific tags deferred to proposals P1/P2; (7) no ambient tags in 0.1; (8) discrepancy routing deterministic on `reference_conforms`; (9) execution timeouts are per-execution payload; (10) hashes use substrate's `sha256:<hex>` format. Grounds: project — v0.2 §3, §3.1, §10, §11.1-11.4, §15; substrate `kernel/sequencer.py:177`, `kernel/topology.py:185-205`; SDD — `grammar/PRINCIPLES.md` commitments 1-3, `grammar/BOOTSTRAP.md` Steps 3, 5, 9; practice — msgspec Struct validation (substrate's declared dependency). Revisit if: substrate changes how it derives event kinds, or guest-trace volume makes per-line JSON too slow (then a binary trace format becomes a Layer-6 proposal).

- **2026-10-05 user** — No physical computer is acquired for this project ("Nothing needs a Windows machine... Nobody needs a physical computer here", chat 2026-10-05). Supersedes the agent-resolved 2026-10-05 "Windows reference machine" entry, which read Q2 as a hardware purchase.
- **2026-10-05 agent-resolved** — How v0.2 §11.1's native x86-64 reference is met without a physical machine. Decision: the reference profile runs Windows on rented x86-64 hardware: a hosted CI runner or a cloud VM. Code there executes natively on x86 CPUs under a hypervisor, which satisfies "native x86-64 Windows" for instruction, ordering, and fault observations. Each reference profile records the hypervisor and the CPU model it ran on. Emulated x86 on this Mac (e.g. QEMU TCG) and Windows on Arm stay non-reference profiles (constraint E4). Nothing in Phase 1 before the first probe needs the instance. Grounds: project — user decision above; v0.2 §11.1; SDD — AGENTS.md resolve-and-record; practice — hosted Windows CI runners and cloud Windows VMs run x86-64 guests on x86-64 hosts (hardware virtualization, not instruction emulation). Revisit if: a probe's observation differs between a virtualized and a bare-metal run (then the profile records which, and the contract states applicability).
- **2026-10-05 agent-resolved** — Contract file format and layout. Decision: JSON decoded strictly by `src/w2m/contract.py` at `contracts/<contract_id>/<version>.json`, evidence embedded in the contract file (each entry keeps its category, source, applicability, and verbatim quote). This supersedes the `contracts/<id>/evidence/` layout named in the `signals/0.1-rationale.md` dual-contract audit table; the vocabulary is unaffected. Grounds: project — v0.2 §11.1 (evidence travels with the definition), §11.2 (YAML fragment is illustrative); SDD — TECHNIQUES.md #44 spirit (one file per concept, edited in place); practice — stdlib `json`, no new dependency. Revisit if: evidence volume per contract makes the file unwieldy (then split evidence out by evidence_id).
- **2026-10-05 agent-resolved** — Role-to-thread mapping for comparisons comes from the probe, passed as `role_map` to the comparator. Sprint 036 fills the decoder for the game: the 123 gap kinds and the 142 unconfirmed rows (criterion (d)). Graphics opens as its own phase, with the DXBC input path decided against DXMT and Metal Shader Converter (which accepts DXIL only). Grounds: project — v0.2 §2, §5, §5.1, §8.2, §10.1, §11.1; review 2026-10-09 §5; SDD — AGENTS.md hard rule 4; practice — Knight and Leveson 1986 (agreement between two implementations of the same rules is not evidence of correctness). - **2026-10-09 agent-resolved** — Runtime architecture (v0.2 §8.2 backend selection; review 2026-10-09 H1). Supersedes "Sprint 034 decides the guest runtime architecture" in the sequence entry above: the evidence was already on the record and leaves one design.
  (1) **Backend: the managed VM.** On this host, guest TSO in the VM runs at 0.99-1.10x native plain access, against 2.64-2.71x for the cheapest native-process lowering (ldapr/stlr) and 7.4x for dmb fences (Sprint 011, record ordering-1791442786); EnTSO gives 0 forbidden MP outcomes in 50M (Sprint 009); the VM enforces 4 KB guest pages in hardware (fault EC 0x25 at A+4096), while a macOS process cannot (mprotect at 4 KB returns EINVAL; Sprint 012); no public native-process TSO control exists in the SDK (Sprint 008). The VM back end already runs lifted code and passes lockstep and the state differential (Sprints 030, 032). Native-process lowering stays a later comparison subject (v0.2 §8.2), not a second build.
  (2) **Windows-visible state lives in guest memory at Windows layouts.** The game's runtime reads it there without calling anything: Mono's JIT reads TLS from `gs:[0x1480 + key*8]` and `gs:[0x1780]` (TEB), its VEH reads and rewrites CONTEXT, Boehm GC and Mono call SuspendThread, GetThreadContext and SetThreadContext. So TEB, PEB, the loader's module list, the VEH chain, and one CPU-state block per thread (GPRs, RIP, flags, XMM/YMM, MXCSR, x87 with its control word, segment bases) are guest memory. Translated code may hold guest registers in host registers; every block carries reconstruction tables (host PC → guest RIP and register locations) so a CONTEXT can be built at any instruction boundary (v0.2 §5).
  (3) **Windows services run inside the guest, as AArch64 code compiled from C** (freestanding, `clang --target=aarch64-none-elf`, no libc), linked into the guest runtime image the monitor loads. Faults, VEH dispatch, TLS, handles, waits, thread suspend and context access never leave the VM. Only host effects cross the boundary — files, time, windows, input, audio, graphics, network — through one typed transport (v0.2 §7, last paragraph), replacing today's text pipe to Python. C, because clang and the AArch64 toolchain are on this host (Xcode 26.6), the monitor and probes are already C, the Windows ABI and its structures are C-shaped, and the reference implementations the project measures against are C (Wine, box64). Every service is a separate compilation unit with one contract, so the candidate loop generates, installs and judges it the way Sprint 031 did SetLastError; Python candidates end with the host kernel32.
  (4) **One vCPU per guest thread**, each on its own host thread (Hypervisor.framework binds a vCPU to the thread that created it); macOS schedules them. SuspendThread forces an exit with `hv_vcpus_exit` and reads the state block; guest TSO holds across them (EnTSO set per vCPU, Sprint 008).
  (5) **Translation**: x86-64 → explicit operations (`lift.py`) → AArch64 (`vm_backend.py`), ahead of time for shipped code and on a dispatch miss for code found or written at run time (v0.2 §6). The translator stays Python on the host behind one interface (guest bytes and address in; AArch64 block, its source range, code version and reconstruction table out), so moving it into the guest later changes no caller. Mono's RWX regions are write-protected after translation; a write faults, invalidates the affected translations, and the next entry retranslates (v0.2 §5.1).
  (6) **Signals**: substrate orchestrates generation and judgment on the harness plane, as now. The guest runtime writes guest-plane events (vocabulary 0.5) into a guest ring buffer the monitor drains to the trace store, so every service call, fault and thread event is judged by the existing comparator. Translation emits `CODE_TARGET_DISCOVERED`, `TRANSLATION_PUBLISHED` and `TRANSLATION_INVALIDATED`, which have no emitter today.
  Grounds: project — v0.2 §5, §5.1, §6, §7, §8.1, §8.2; Sprints 008, 009, 011, 012, 030-033 measurements; Unity-Technologies/mono `unity-2019.4-mbe` source (read 2026-10-09); SDD — AGENTS.md resolve-and-record, hard rule 4; practice — box64 and Wine keep Windows structures in guest-visible memory and write their runtimes in C; Rosetta 2 and FEX reconstruct x86 state from host state at fault points. - **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The form of the compiler inside the VM (recentering step 6). Decision: copy-and-patch at the encoder level. A stencil is a sequence of arm64 encoder calls whose arguments are expressions of the instruction's guest registers and values; the stencils come from the Python compiler (the rules and `compile_insn`), captured per form and value class by the census's method, never written by hand; the compiler in the VM is a generated C decoder (from `specs/x86_decode.json`), the form key, the stencil table, the encoders in C (judged against `a64_encoder.py` by a differential) and the hole expressions; a form with no stencil exits to the host, which compiles it in Python as now and counts it. The words of every instruction compiled in the VM must equal the Python compiler's, over the census record and the class runs. Grounds: project — Sprint 071's census (`scripts/stencil_census.py` over frontier-643a6283510c record of the batch run to 100 billion instructions, cold): 1,313,377 compiled instructions, every one rebuilt exactly from its logged encoder calls; with value classes (zero, sign, the 12-bit and 9-bit ranges, the scaled offset per access size, nonzero halfwords, a shift count's 1 and 0) all 18,218 forms produce one encoder sequence; 1,287 distinct sequences, the commonest 100 covering 93.2% of compiled instructions; every hole a register, a value, a piece of one, its negation or an affine function of one (70 cases: 16 times an XMM number, 64 or 32 less a shift count). Industry — Xu and Kjolstad, "Copy-and-Patch Compilation", OOPSLA 2021 (1,666 stencils for WebAssembly); CPython 3.13's JIT, PEP 744, whose stencils are built from the same definitions as its interpreter, so one change updates both. Open, for the next sprints: 394,155 words (in 364,521 instructions) are built inline in `vm_backend.py` rather than by an encoder (ADD/SUB immediates, the flag sequences), 1,833 of their positions holding holes; they move into encoder functions first, with the words unchanged. The census covers the start-up's first 100 billion instructions; forms first met later (mono's run-time code in play) take the host path until a stencil exists for them.
- **2026-10-14 agent-resolved** — The stencil key of the compiler inside the VM, and how its holes are found (refines the copy-and-patch Decision above). Decision: a stencil is keyed by what the generated decoder gives (the spec row, iclass, operand and address size, VEX.L, the operands with register numbers and values taken out, which registers are equal, which explicit register is one of the row's implicit registers, the value classes of the immediates, displacements, the next address and any named address) and the compile call's flag arguments; the C decoder therefore outputs `x86dec.Fields` and `x86ops` operands, and a generated table gives each row's implicit registers. Keys come from recorded runs; each key's holes and their expressions come from compiling instances made for that key (registers and values changed within their classes), not from a run's variation. Grounds: project — Sprint 074 (`scripts/stencil_census.py --decoder-key` over a cold batch run's record of 1,313,377 compiled instructions): 38,154 keys, every one one encoder sequence; every hole that varies named by one expression of one decoded value over all its form's instructions; 17,276 keys seen with one distinct instruction, so the record leaves their holes unshown; the first key without implicit registers split a MUL row (MUL RDX). Practice — copy-and-patch derives each stencil's holes from the stencil's construction, not from observed uses (Xu and Kjolstad, OOPSLA 2021: a stencil's holes are the relocations in its object code, known from how the stencil was built). Revisit if: a key gives more than one sequence on a made instance.
- **2026-10-15 agent-resolved** — How the stencil table holds where its samples are thin (amends Decision 2026-10-14, the stencil key; review 2026-10-15 M4). Decision: (1) a key whose made instances give two sequences is refined by the first feature of one decoded value that separates them (at least 8, 16, 32, 64 or 128; the low 1-5 bits), from a per-key entry (Sprint 076, 58 keys); (2) the key's flag arguments are canonical: `live_after` and `nzcv_out` are kept only for an instruction with a flags op and `nzcv_from` only for a Jcc its kind's NZCV_CONDITIONS holds, since compile_insn reads them nowhere else (Sprint 079: 38,154 keys to 21,182, none split; the review's E2: 199,826 non-canonical argument sets, 0 differing words); (3) the table emits only what its samples prove: every value the samples never varied is guarded (an instruction whose value there differs goes to the host), a register hole is read only through the register map, and a value hole that a second expression also fits is checked at emit time, the instruction refused where they disagree (Sprint 080). Grounds: project — Sprint 079's held-out judge found 144 wrong compiles on 078's table, where a constant stood for a value no sample varied; Sprint 080's measures; SDD — KIT_DIARY #58, #59; practice — none outside the project for inferred stencils (copy-and-patch knows its holes by construction). Revisit if: a judge from outside the table's sources finds a differing word.

- **2026-10-16 user** — Windows's own code as the specification (chat, 2026-10-16: "Every Windows function is knowable. We can build something that knows all that and then just closes those tiny gaps ... We need to pivot and solve this immediately"; "Solving does not mean doing. Things are fully solved, then they are done"; then "go" on the plan). Decision: w2m stops rewriting Windows's API and runs Microsoft's own user-mode DLLs (the laptop's System32, SysWOW64 and WinSxS, build 26200), translated like game code; w2m writes only the edge beneath them: the NT system calls with their information classes (ntdll 488 numbers, win32u 1,486), the state the kernel builds (the API set map, \KnownDlls, KUSER_SHARED_DATA), the kernel's three entries into user mode, csrss's base-server messages, presentation to a Metal layer, WASAPI on Core Audio, one Metal user-mode display driver per Direct3D driver interface with DXBC through dxilconv and DXIL through Metal Shader Converter, a generated Steam bridge, and the 32-bit mode switch under Microsoft's wow64 DLLs. The frontier is re-aimed, not retired: its stops become system-call, win32k and driver-interface differences against the laptop. The old path stays the game's path of record until the new one passes it. Excluded from "any game": kernel-mode anti-cheat. The whole plan, each layer's source, edge, design, judge and the experiments left after public sources were searched: docs/solution-2026-10-16.md. Grounds: project — 338 hand-written services against 120,902 exports; the user decisions of 2026-10-16 (any game, at speed; knowable first); practice — Drawbridge ran Windows 7 user mode on a small host interface (Porter et al., ASPLOS 2011); Mesa's d3d10umd ran under Microsoft's runtime and passed the HCK wgf11 tests; Proton's lsteamclient is generated from the Steamworks headers. - **2026-10-17 agent-resolved** — Sprint 117 (chain step 4): what a kick is and what the host does with one. Decision: a kick is any change of the channel page's kick word (offset 2048, one 32-bit store), so kicks made before the monitor's kicker looks are one; a canceled exit of a kicked vCPU reports kind `kick` ahead of the watchdog's `cancel` (a deadline passed with it waits for the next run's); `serve_exit` answers a kick by resuming the vCPU where it stopped and counts it in `VM.kicks`, and what the kick was for is the kicking thread's to act on at the run loop's top (chain steps 5 and 6). Grounds: project — `docs/design-several-vcpus.md` ("A stop and a preemption (SuspendThread on a thread another vCPU runs) are kicks"); the channel's sequence words, written with one store since 2026-10-14; SDD — hard rule 6 as the project applies it; practice — Apple's hv_vcpu.h (`hv_vcpus_exit` names no owning thread; a vCPU not running returns at once from its next hv_vcpu_run). - **2026-10-17 agent-resolved** — Sprint 118 (chain step 5): how one process's threads run on several vCPUs, and what judges it. Decision: a run entry's free word (0x138) marks the vCPU that has the thread loaded, set at a load and cleared at a save by the guest's `w2m_switch` and the host's `_load_thread`/`_save_thread`, and both switches skip a thread another vCPU holds; with `VM.thread_vcpus` > 1 a host thread serves each vCPU past the first under the shared runtime lock, which covers each serve and the words written before a run; a vCPU past the first with no thread parks (gives the lock back, waits on the VM's condition, which each serve notifies while one waits), and vCPU 0 never parks: with no thread while another vCPU runs one it waits holding its own, and otherwise keeps today's path (idle to the Machine, warp, sleep, or the deadlock), so deadlines stay one vCPU's to keep. The judge is the wait_handoff family, not the threads family the design named: the threads probe suspends a spinning thread, which on two vCPUs runs elsewhere (chain step 6). The guest's and host's take of the runtime lock become compare-and-swap (CASA; `__atomic_compare_exchange_n`): Sprint 106's exchange wrote each waiter's value over the holder's, so the word named the last waiter, and RuntimeLock's "the vCPU holds it already" read a waiter as the holder (the first two-vCPU run hung with both host threads in the take, the word 1). - **2026-10-18 agent-resolved** — Sprint 119 (chain step 6, the chain's last): SuspendThread and GetThreadContext on a thread another vCPU runs. Windows' SuspendThread may return before the thread stops and GetThreadContext completes the stop; returning after is one of the outcomes Windows allows, and suspend.frozen needs the thread stopped by the row. vCPU 0, with no thread to run while another vCPU runs one, puts its thread down before it waits, notifies, and carries that (`saved`) through the Machine's idle turn; every pick of a thread to load skips one another vCPU holds; on several vCPUs a thread put down has its vectors written to its slot at once. Grounds: project — `docs/design-several-vcpus.md` chain step 6; probes/threads/threads.c (suspend, suspend.frozen, context) and probes/running_thread_context; a trace of every save and load (main resumed from stale registers after an idle turn); SDD — hard rule 6 as the project applies it; practice — Microsoft's GetThreadContext page: "You cannot get a valid context for a running thread. Use the SuspendThread function to suspend the thread before calling GetThreadContext" (contracts/windows.thread.running_context). Revisit if: a program suspends threads from two vCPUs at each other (both hosts would wait), or a measure needs vCPU 0 to keep deadlines while it waits.

- **2026-10-18 agent-resolved** — Sprint 120: every timed wait ends, the class Sprint 119 deferred one member of. Decision: the VM and the host make expired waits ready on every clock, the host reading the guest's counter as time.monotonic (mach_absolute_time, measured equal to CNTVCT_EL0 on vCPU 0 and vCPU 1); every host wait times out at the earliest deadline, and a whole second of every vCPU waiting with no deadline and no notify stops the run as a deadlock; a suspend notifies, and a waiter whose own thread is suspended stops waiting (SuspendThread returns and yields, GetThreadContext is retried after the resume), so no waiter holds a suspended thread; a second process starts time slices for a process of one thread; a process whose deadline passed is ready to the Machine; on the instruction clock a process takes the machine's latest clock at its start and on each resume; `wake_all` notifies. Refused with Unimplemented: the instruction or host clock with threads on several vCPUs, and vCPU 0 waiting for another vCPU while another process could run, which the game reaches (it always starts UnityCrashHandler64.exe), so step 7 needs the Machine driven from elsewhere before it runs the game. The batch run's calls move: the crash handler gets turns and a clock that keeps up (the review of 2026-10-18 reverted one change at a time: the second process's slices and the catch-up together are the cause); it still conforms, and its instruction count at the stop is no invariant (80.7 to 83.0 billion over Sprints 114-119, the stop tested on wall-clock progress lines). Grounds: project — eight guests in tests/data/timed_waits, each hang measured before its fix and each fix's plant measured to fail; SDD — Decision 2026-10-18 user; practice — Microsoft's SuspendThread is asynchronous and a suspended thread stops on its return to user mode, which the retry departs from (the CONTEXT is written after the resume; no row reads it in between). Revisit if: a row reads a CONTEXT buffer while its writer is suspended, or step 7 moves the Machine's driver.
- **2026-10-21 agent-resolved** — Sprint 121: how a vCPU waiting in the host for a thread learns of one the guest readies inside the VM (Sprint 118's Deferred entry, made this sprint's scope by Sprint 120). Decision: the run table's word 0x28 (RT_WAITING, unused since Sprint 107) counts the vCPUs waiting to take a thread (`_park`, vCPU 0 in `_switch`; not `_await_put_down`, which takes none), written by the host under the runtime lock as each starts and, having the lock again, ends its wait; threads.c `readied`, after `w2m_signal` or `w2m_ready_thread` readies a thread, asks the host (W2M_THREAD_WAKE, which notifies the waiters) while the count is above 0, and otherwise ends the running thread's slice as before; the host, resuming the readying vCPU, ends its slice only for ready threads beyond the waiting vCPUs (`_slice_until`), the one decider. A first draft had the guest claim waiting vCPUs and decide the slice itself; the host's resume rewrites SLICE_UNTIL after every request, so the guest's decision never stood (a plant of the claim changed nothing in 10 runs), and the rule moved to the host. An SGI through the GIC is not built: Hypervisor.framework returns from hv_vcpu_run on a guest WFI, so an idle vCPU waits in the host either way. Grounds: project — the readied guest (worker rounds on the waiting vCPU in 3 runs of 13 before, 10 of 10 after, no switch in the VM) and twoready guest, tests/test_parked_vcpu_wake.py; docs/design-several-vcpus.md (a wake from outside the host's serves notifies); SDD — Decision 2026-10-11 user; practice — QEMU target/arm/hvf/hvf.c (`EC_WFX_TRAP`, `hvf_wfi` halts the vCPU thread in the host). Revisit if: the wake requests take a measured share of a run, or a vCPU must wake from a timer interrupt.
- **2026-10-21 agent-resolved** — Sprint 122: how a process's threads on several vCPUs share the one thread that drives a kernel.Machine (step 7 of the several-vCPU chain; Sprint 120's refusal). Decision: in a Machine, vCPU 0 never waits for another vCPU: with no thread to take it puts its thread down, counts itself in RT_WAITING and answers IDLE, and the Machine runs another process, or waits on its own condition (notified by every serve on a host thread) until a change, the earliest deadline or its one-second stop check, stopping as a deadlock only after a whole second with no change, no deadline and no host thread running; a process is ready to the Machine only for a thread no other vCPU holds. The Machine's tables are under one lock. A wake from a thread that does not serve a process is left pending and applied under that process's runtime lock at an exit from outside the runtime's C, and every slice of the process ends at the host until then; a wake made on a host thread kicks the process the Machine's thread runs. Every process on one vCPU keeps the old path: one thread, wakes written at once. Found on the way and fixed: a thread sliced out for another process ran again first (a readied thread starved beside a spinning child, on one vCPU); the main process's run was left open when a child stopped; a host request served for vCPU 1 read and wrote vCPU 0's runtime stack (design B's private pages), which stopped the batch run on two vCPUs. Design B, a driver thread per process, was not built: it needs the same pending wake and lock and removes the round robin the instruction clock's runs depend on. Grounds: project — guests handshake, busychild, crosswake, pingpong and statsize (tests/test_processes_beside_vcpus.py), each failure measured before its fix and each plant measured to fail but the direct wake (not caught in 40,000 cross-process wakes); the batch run on two vCPUs conforms (168 lines); SDD — Decisions 2026-10-11 and 2026-10-18 user; practice — Ousterhout, A Philosophy of Software Design ch. 11 (design it twice); Apple's hv_vcpu.h (`hv_vcpus_exit` from any thread; a vCPU not running returns at once from its next run). Revisit if: the driver's hand-overs take a measured share of a run, or a child process runs on several vCPUs.

- **2026-10-23 agent-resolved** — How Rust w2m reaches substrate with no Python of its own. Every tag outside the `guest` category is a substrate application event: a frozen msgspec Struct emitted by a Producer, a Python callable `(input) -> AsyncIterable[Event]`, registered with `producer_kind(schemas=...)` on a `TopologyBuilder` that Python code builds (substrate `docs/api.md` lines 50-63, 280; `signals/0.9.json` `transport.harness_plane`). Substrate has no producer that runs another process, and `substrate run` takes only topologies registered in Python (its CLI; bundles describe chat sessions, not topologies). Decision: substrate gains, on its `w2m` branch, two language-neutral features: (1) a process producer that runs a command, reads one JSON event a line from its standard output (`{"kind": ..., "payload": {...}}`), checks each against schemas read from a JSON file (built as msgspec Structs at load), and emits it, a malformed line ending the producer as failed; (2) a topology read from a file: its producer kinds (each a command and its schema file), initial producers, triggers by kind, and termination by substrate's named recipes, run by `substrate run --file`. w2m's Rust writes the schema file from `signals/<version>.json` and its topology files; no Python file is added to w2m. Rejected: a thin Python adapter in w2m (new Python, Decision 2026-10-23 user); Rust writing substrate's record format itself (a second implementation of its sequencer, CRC framing and hashing, which substrate's replay would then have to trust). Grounds: project — the two gaps read in substrate's API and CLI; SDD — Decisions 2026-10-22 user (substrate stays) and 2026-10-23 user (no Python in w2m); practice — one implementation of a record format, reached across a process boundary by a line protocol, as the guest plane already reaches it (`transport.guest_plane`: a JSONL trace ingested by an operator). Revisit if: substrate's maintainers (the user) want the features elsewhere than the `w2m` branch, or a record from a process producer fails substrate's replay.
- **2026-10-24 agent-resolved** — How Rust w2m reads JSON: `serde_json` 1.0.154 (MIT OR Apache-2.0, crates.io 2026-10-11), its `Value` only, no `serde` derive; writing stays hand-written. Its normal dependencies are `serde`, `serde_core`, `itoa`, `memchr`, `zmij`; `serde_derive` is optional in `serde` and left off, so `syn`, `quote` and `proc-macro2` are not built. RustSec's advisory database has no entry for any of the six (`advisory-db/crates`, read 2026-10-24). Read in its source (the .crate from static.crates.io): an integer is kept exact as u64 or i64 and becomes f64 only past those ranges (`de.rs` `parse_integer`), which holds the guest plane's 64-bit values; a duplicate key keeps the last value (`value/de.rs` `visit_map`), which substrate's records cannot carry (RFC 8785); nesting stops at 128 (`remaining_depth`); objects are a `BTreeMap`, so key order is sorted, and every order the generator depends on comes from a JSON array. Grounds: project — the vocabulary generator (Sprint 126) and every judge of step 2 read JSON (`signals/`, substrate records, `specs/golden/`); SDD — WORKING_AGREEMENT permitted dependencies (a new one is a recorded decision first); practice — a hand-written parser is where JSON code fails (surrogate pairs, number edges), and serde_json's parser is the one read above. Revisit if: a judge needs integers past 64 bits exact (`arbitrary_precision`), or typed decoding enough to want `serde_derive`, each its own decision.

- **2026-10-24 agent-resolved** — Architecture: the Rust host's locks (Sprint 130; fix (b) of Decision 2026-10-24 user), in `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-24.md`, which replaces `docs/design-rust-host-2026-10-23.md`. Decision: no thread waits, on any service path, for a lock another thread's service holds. (1) Each service the guest runtime answers takes the lock of what it touches or none: none for the thread-local calls and QueryPerformanceCounter (the three hottest imports, about 154,000, 129,000 and 94,000 calls a second in the batch run), a lock per heap as Windows has, the TLS slots, the handle table, each sync object, the modules, the call trace's atomic sequence, and the run-table word for the run table only, a leaf. (2) A transport page per vCPU, and no guest lock across `hvc #8`; each of the 65 `w2m_host` call sites is read for state it holds across the request. (3) Translation into a region per vCPU, with its own cursor, save area and runtime stack; dispatch slots read and written as atomic pairs or under a sequence word, filled by compare-and-swap, removed by tombstone; a lock per guest code page for invalidation; reclamation by per-vCPU epochs. (4) One process's objects and waits stay in its VM; the supervisor holds only what crosses processes. (5) Every lock word has Drepper's three states, a bounded spin, then a wait in the host on the word's address. Host locks are each held for one access, `files` only for the descriptor table; `modules` across one map, as Windows's loader lock is. The run-table word stays one lock for scheduling, about 31,000 takings a second in play, until the scheduler's port. Grounds: project — `vm_backend.py` 3762-3765, 3809, 4240-4251; `runtime.c` 25; Decision 2026-10-09 (hot imports); the reviewer's two passes; SDD — AGENTS.md hard rule 4; practice — QEMU's MTTCG (commit 0ac20318ce16, `docs/devel/multi-thread-tcg.rst`), crosvm's per-device mutex, Wine's esync to ntsync, Microsoft's HeapCreate and loader-lock pages, Drepper's "Futexes Are Tricky", Uhlig et al. (VM '04), the Arm ARM's concurrent-modification rule, Intel's cross-modifying-code rule, Go's per-P run queue. Revisit if: step 6 measures the run-table word busy more than 5% of the time or a p99 wait above 10 µs (then per-vCPU run queues), or a play stretch sends the supervisor more than 376 requests a second (then shared-page cross-process objects).

## Deferred

- **2026-10-03 (Agent)** — Steam acquisition (`w2m steam install`) and the lsteamclient-style Steam bridge. Re-visit when the v0 slice launches a locally supplied game directory. *(Withdrawn 2026-10-03 by the v0.2 user decision: §4.2 makes the bridge a first-class subsystem and §12.1 puts acquisition inside the product slice.)*
- **2026-10-03 (Agent)** — Native-process backend and AOT cache. Re-visit when the VM backend passes the slice's observation contract. *(Withdrawn 2026-10-03 by the v0.2 user decision: both backends are candidates measured against one contract, §8.2.)*

---

- **2026-10-07 (Agent)** — Full x86-TSO litmus coverage on the VM backend (SB must stay observable, LB/2+2W/IRIW must stay forbidden) using herdtools7's x86 tests as the model reference. Re-visit when the CPU translator emits guest code for these tests.

- **2026-10-08 (Agent)** — Hoisted page-protection checks need an invalidation rule against concurrent VirtualProtect (protection epoch or re-translation). Re-visit when the native-process backend compiles its first loop with hoisted checks.

- **2026-10-08 (Agent)** — Static traversal walks into data embedded in hand-written assembly (OpenSSL credit strings in libcrypto `.text`). Re-visit when the traversal feeds the translator: refuse-by-all-decoders marks a data region.

- **2026-10-08 (Agent) — RESOLVED 2026-10-08, Sprint 028 (witness in the trace; contract 0.3.1)** — The event probe's precondition (both waiters inside `WaitForSingleObject` when `SetEvent` runs) rests on `Sleep(200)` and is not witnessed (review §4.7). The 10/10 release of waiter a fits FIFO selection and fits waiter b sometimes not yet waiting. Candidate witness (unverified, needs a doc check): read each waiter thread's state and wait reason from `NtQuerySystemInformation(SystemProcessInformation)` just before `SetEvent` and record it in the trace. Re-visit at the next runner sprint for this contract; the contract's acceptance set {a},{b} is unaffected, its 'observed' evidence of FIFO-looking order is.

---

- **2026-10-11** — Families in parallel worktrees (Sprint 049 card item 5, Decision 2026-10-10 point 2): Sprint 049 ran its families one after another in one session. Re-visit when a sprint opens three or more independent families at once. *(Withdrawn 2026-10-11: carried out from Sprint 062, Decision 2026-10-11 agent-resolved on parallel worktrees.)*
- **2026-10-11** — The win32metadata reader (Sprint 049 card item 6): no family needed a generated skeleton. Re-visit when a family's probe needs more than ten functions' signatures.

- **2026-10-11** — Native lowerings for set 050's classes: all 64 run in the model inside the VM (hvc #10, #11), as the VEX classes of 049 and 049b partly do. Re-visit when a frontier or play run measures a share of its time in model steps. *(Moved to plan row 056, Decision 2026-10-11 user.)*
- **2026-10-11** — MMX and x87 (the MMX-register forms of MOVD, MOVQ, PADDW, PUNPCKLBW and the rest, EMMS, MOVDQ2Q; FLDCW, FNCLEX, FNSTSW, FWAIT, FISTP, FIADD, FADD, FIDIVR): one register file, one later set. Re-visit when the frontier reaches one. *(Moved to plan row 054, Decision 2026-10-11 user.)*

- **2026-10-11** — The game's collector path (SuspendThread, GetThreadContext, ResumeThread on mono's threads): judged by the threads rows, not yet run by the game. Re-visit when the frontier reaches mono's first collection. *(Moved to plan row 055, Decision 2026-10-11 user.)*

- **2026-10-14 (Agent)** — A fault counts its block's instructions after it: the block's entry charged them, and the runtime's exception path (EL1 vector, exception entry, the handler or the unhandled stop) leaves the block without the host giving them back, as it now does after a model step (3d052448). The instruction clock runs ahead by those instructions at each fault. Re-visit when the exception entry gives back the unrun instructions (the tail table, tail_hosts, is the host's; the entry would need its own), or when a contract value is traced to a fault's clock.

## Open questions

- **OQ-1.** Is `ACTLR_EL1.TSOEN` settable for a guest vCPU through public Hypervisor.framework on macOS 26, or only through a private path? Settled by a probe sprint, not by reading. — **Answered 2026-10-07 (Sprint 008):** public. `HV_SYS_REG_ACTLR_EL1` (macOS 15.0+, SDK header `hv_vcpu_types.h`: "only allows getting / setting of the ACTLR_EL1.EnTSO bit (index 1)"); on Mac17,7 / macOS 25F80 the bit sets, reads back 0x2, and survives an `hv_vcpu_run` exit. Still open: the ordering *effect* (a two-vCPU litmus test). — **Effect demonstrated 2026-10-07 (Sprint 009):** MP litmus, 2 vCPUs, guest MMU on with Normal WB memory: EnTSO off 2245 / 50,000,000 forbidden outcomes; EnTSO on 0 / 50,000,000 (95% upper bound 6.0e-08).
- **OQ-3.** (Answered with OQ-1: the custom-guest path is public Hypervisor.framework, not a Virtualization.framework/Rosetta-only facility.) v0.2 §8.1 cites Apple's "Accelerating the performance of Rosetta" for guest `ACTLR.TSOEN` control. That page describes Rosetta inside a Linux guest under Virtualization.framework; whether a custom compact guest under Hypervisor.framework gets the same control is OQ-1, settled by the capability probe v0.2 §8.1 calls for.
- **OQ-2.** Which Metal Shader Converter version and license terms apply to redistributing its output inside a generated app bundle?

---
- **2026-10-08 (Sprint 031)** — `windows.thread.last_error.set_get@0.1.0` accepts a candidate whose GetLastError returns the constant 1234: its one scenario fixes the value at build time. What scenario and relation would make the value unpredictable to a candidate (a run-time value recorded in the trace, judged as "GetLastError returns what SetLastError was given")? Pinned by `test_one_scenario_contract_accepts_a_constant`.
- **2026-10-09 (Sprint 032)** — Answered for the Sprint 031 question above: version 0.2.0 picks three values at run time (QueryPerformanceCounter into a 256-entry table that carries each value's args_sha256) and judges with `exact` (GetLastError returns the argument of the latest SetLastError, checked by hash). The constant is rejected under 0.2.0; `test_constant_rejected_under_0_2_0_accepted_under_0_1_0` holds both verdicts.
- **2026-10-09 (substrate, upstream)** — `substrate.api.Producer` declares its events as `substrate.types.Event` (the bus frame), while the kernel takes and checks the producer's own msgspec Structs. A fix belongs in laffeyp/substrate (`AsyncIterable[Struct]`); w2m bridges it in `w2m.record.producer` until then.
- **2026-10-10 (review 2026-10-10 D8)** — The local VM needs internet when it runs the Steam client so `SteamAPI_Init` succeeds (Decision 2026-10-09, the VM's role), while the guest's lack of internet is "not a defect" for driving it (user, chat 2026-10-09). No sprint has needed Steam in the VM yet (local-VM review L3 open). Raise with the user when one does.
- **2026-10-16 (Sprint 089)** — Does lift() address through R8D-R15D under the 0x67 prefix with 64 bits? `_operand` sets 32-bit addressing only for an E-named base (or an E-named index with no base); the SDM (Vol 1 3.3.7) computes any 32-bit address-size effective address in 32 bits. The class case sets hold 11,202 such cases, 944 with the base's upper half nonzero, and their classes are accepted against Intel's results: evidence against, not proof (both addresses may fault, or reach the same bytes). Settled by examining those 944 cases' accesses, or by a probe on the laptop's x86 processor.

- **2026-10-16 (full review, item 6)** — Milestone C's bound. The review marks plan.md's "frames under 16.7 ms" as stale; the paste cut the text that replaces it. What is milestone C's speed bound (the game's own 113 frames a second on the laptop, 60, or another figure)? — **Answered 2026-10-16 (user):** no bound; as fast as possible, already the user decision of 2026-10-14.
